      m₂ (cStack (q + S.F)) = wd W ((pos + callSize S.F args : ℕ) : ℤ) ∧
      AgreeOutside (Writable S.toSizes (q + S.F)) m m₂ := by
  obtain ⟨-, hlen, hwa⟩ := Stmt.width_call_le_iff.1 B.width
  obtain ⟨frameCode, jumpCode, -⟩ := codeAt_call B.codeAt
  have hpos : pos + (callSize S.F args + 2) ≤ S.disp := B.below
  rw [List.getD_eq_getElem?_getD, he, Option.getD_some] at jumpCode
  -- the arguments: there are at most F of them, each of width at most F
  obtain ⟨vals, A⟩ := effects_argsCode B.toFramed args 0 hwa hsa (by omega)
  have A₁ := A.mono temps_subset_scratch
  -- the new frame
  obtain ⟨R₂, hret, A₂⟩ := effects_newFrame (B.toFramed.same A₁)
    ((Nat.le_add_right _ _).trans (B.room_succ hd)) args (by simpa using vals)
    (ret := pos + callSize S.F args) (by omega)
  have run₂ := steps_straight _ pos m frameCode
    (List.forall_mem_append.2 ⟨straight_argsCode _ _, straight_newFrame _ _ _⟩)
  rw [effects_append, List.length_append, length_argsCode, length_newFrame] at run₂
  -- the jump
  exact ⟨_, (run₂.trans (steps_jump jumpCode R₂.neg S.inRange_neg_one)).cast_count
    (by rw [callSize]; omega), R₂, hret, (A₁.mono scratch_subset_writable).trans A₂⟩

/-- Leaving a procedure whose frame is q + F + 1, when the stack cell before that frame holds the
position ret: the return sequence and the dispatcher.  The machine arrives at ret; RET holds the
result (local variable 0), FP points to the frame q, and otherwise only scratch cells have
changed. -/
private theorem steps_callLeave (S : Setting W) {ret : ℕ} (R : Rel S.toSizes σ (q + S.F + 1) m)
    (hcode : CodeAt S.code pos (epilogue S.toCodeLayout))
    (hret : m (cStack (q + S.F)) = wd W ret) (hlt : ret < S.disp) (hQ : q + S.F + 1 ≤ S.Q)
    (hF : 0 < S.F) :
    ∃ m', Steps S.code (epilogueSize + 2 * (ret + 1)) ⟨pos, m⟩ ⟨ret, m'⟩ ∧
      m' cFP = wd W (cStack q) ∧ m' cRET = wd W (σ.loc 0) ∧
      AgreeOutside (insert cFP (Scratch S.F)) m m' := by
  have hN := S.offsets_le
  -- RET := the cell at FP, which is local variable 0
  have run₁ := steps_load (i := cRET) hcode R.fp (S.inRange_stack hQ)
  rw [R.loc_zero hF] at run₁
  have A₁ := (AgreeOutside.refl (Scratch S.F) m).update (a := cRET) (by cells) (wd W (σ.loc 0))
  -- ADDR := FP + 2, the address of the stack cell before the frame
  have run₂ := run₁.trans (steps_add hcode.tail (R.same A₁).fp ((R.same A₁).pool 2 (by omega)))
  rw [cStack_succ_add] at run₂
  have A₂ := A₁.update (a := cADDR) (by cells) (wd W (cStack (q + S.F)))
  -- RR := the cell at ADDR, which holds ret
  have run₃ := run₂.trans (steps_load hcode.tail.tail (Function.update_self _ _ _)
    (S.inRange_stack (by omega)))
  rw [A₂.read (by cells) hret] at run₃
  have A₃ := A₂.update (a := cRR) (by cells) (wd W ret)
  -- FP := FP + 2 (F + 1), the frame of the caller
  have run₄ := run₃.trans (steps_add hcode.tail.tail.tail (R.same A₃).fp ((R.same A₃).pool _ hN))
  rw [Nat.add_assoc q, cStack_add] at run₄
  -- the jump to the dispatcher
  have run₅ := run₄.trans (steps_jump hcode.tail.tail.tail.tail
    (by cell_read using (R.same A₃).neg) S.inRange_neg_one)
  -- the dispatcher, which leaves -1 in RR
  have run₆ := run₅.trans (steps_dispatcher S.dispatcherAt hlt S.fits.pool_lt _
    (by cell_read using (R.same A₃).one) (by cell_read))
  exact ⟨_, run₆, by cell_read, by cell_read,
    ((A₃.mono (by cells)).update (by cells) _).update (by cells) _⟩

/-- x := p(args): enter, the body, leave, store the result. -/
private theorem sim_call {body : Stmt} {k : ℕ} (hsa : ∀ e ∈ args, e.Safe S.lim σ)
    (hp : S.P[p]? = some body) (hd : d < S.lim.depth)
    (ih : Simulates S (d + 1) body ⟨frame (args.map (·.val σ)), σ.mem⟩ σ' k) :
    Simulates S d (.call p args x) σ ⟨Function.update σ.loc x (σ'.loc 0), σ'.mem⟩
      ((args.map Expr.cost).sum + 2 + k) := by
  intro pos q m B
  obtain ⟨hx, -, -⟩ := Stmt.width_call_le_iff.1 B.width
  obtain ⟨e, he, procCode, hend⟩ := S.bodyAt p body hp
  obtain ⟨-, -, resultCode⟩ := codeAt_call B.codeAt
  have hpos : pos + (callSize S.F args + 2) ≤ S.disp := B.below
  have hq := B.low
  have hroom := B.room_succ hd
  -- enter
  obtain ⟨m₁, run₁, R₁, hret, A₁⟩ := steps_callEnter B hsa hd he
  -- the body, in the frame q + F + 1
  obtain ⟨m₂, n₂, run₂, le₂, R₂, A₂⟩ := ih e (q + S.F + 1) m₁
    { rel := R₁, low := by omega, high := by omega, codeAt := procCode.left, below := by omega,
      width := S.width body (List.mem_of_getElem? hp),
      bounded := bounded_callFrame B.bounded args hsa, room := hroom }
  -- the body writes the stack only from its frame on, so the position to return to is still there
  have hret₂ := A₂.read (a := cStack (q + S.F)) (by cells) hret
  -- leave
  obtain ⟨m₃, run₃, hfp, hres, A₃⟩ := steps_callLeave S R₂
    (procCode.right.cast_pos (by rw [length_compileStmt])) hret₂ (by omega) (by omega)
    (Nat.zero_lt_of_lt hx)
  -- since the call began, the stack has changed only from cell q + F on: the local variables of the
  -- caller are as before, and the memory is as the procedure left it
  have A : AgreeOutside (Writable S.toSizes (q + S.F)) m m₃ :=
    (A₁.trans (A₂.mono (writable_subset_writable (by omega)))).trans
      (A₃.mono (Set.insert_subset (by cells) scratch_subset_writable))
  have I₃ : Framed S.toSizes ⟨σ.loc, σ'.mem⟩ q m₃ := ⟨R₂.of_agreeOutside A₃ (by cells) hfp
    (fun y hy => A.read (by cells) (B.rel.loc y hy))
    fun a ha => A₃.read (by cells) (R₂.mem a ha), hq, B.high⟩
  -- store the result
  obtain ⟨m₄, run₄, R₄, A₄⟩ := steps_setVar I₃ hx resultCode hres (by cells)
  exact ⟨m₄, _, {
    run := (((run₁.trans run₂).trans run₃).trans run₄).cast_pos (by rw [Stmt.size, Nat.add_assoc])
    le := call_le_stepsPerStep_mul hpos (by omega) le₂
    rel := R₄
    agree := (A.mono (writable_subset_writable (by omega))).trans A₄ }⟩

end call

/-! ## The theorem -/

/-- **The simulation theorem.**  The code of a statement matches every run of the statement. -/
theorem sim (S : Setting W) {c : ℕ} (h : Exec S.lim S.P d s σ σ' c) :
    Simulates S d s σ σ' c := by
  induction h with
  | skip => exact sim_skip
  | set hs => exact sim_set hs
  | store hsa hse haddr => exact sim_store hsa hse haddr
  | seq h₁ _ ih₁ ih₂ => exact sim_seq h₁ ih₁ ih₂
  | iteTrue hs holds _ ih => exact sim_iteTrue hs holds ih
  | iteFalse hs fails _ ih => exact sim_iteFalse hs fails ih
  | whileFalse hs fails => exact sim_whileFalse hs fails
  | whileTrue hs holds h₁ _ ih₁ ih₂ => exact sim_whileTrue hs holds h₁ ih₁ ih₂
  | call hsa hp hd _ ih => exact sim_call hsa hp hd ih

end Light.Compiler
end
```

## ===== TCSlib/Programs/Light/Compiler/ProgramCode.lean =====

```
/-
Copyright (c) 2026 Anthropic, PBC. All rights reserved.
Released under Apache 2.0 license as described in the file LICENSE.
SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0

Vendored from [AFM26] anthropics/formal-math (https://github.com/anthropics/formal-math), folder
`3sum-apsp`, file `ThreeSumApsp/Lang/Compiler/ProgramCode.lean`, at commit e1a4e6508154ea59f030480661590a9fe3018011 (2026-10-05).
Local modifications (see policy.md §2, vendored code, and the repository NOTICE):
* removed the Lean module-system syntax (`module`, `public import`, `@[expose] public section`)
  for compatibility with our v4.25.0 toolchain;
* moved to `TCSlib/Programs/Light/Compiler/ProgramCode.lean`, with imports remapped;
* namespaces `EndStatement` and `ThreeSumApsp.WordRam` renamed to `WordRAM`, and `ThreeSumApsp` to `Light`, with module names and paths in comments updated;
* added a `## References` section citing [AFM26];
* added the repository-standard `set_option` header.
The remaining content is unchanged.
-/
import TCSlib.Programs.Light.Compiler.Dispatcher

set_option maxHeartbeats 0
set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
set_option autoImplicit false

/-!
# The code of a whole program

`compileProgram P p0 dec` is the program of the word RAM that runs procedure p0 of the light
program P.  With dec = false it accepts at the end of the run; with dec = true it accepts if the
result of the procedure is positive and rejects if not.  It has five parts:

| position | part |
|---|---|
| 0 | `headCode`: the numbers 1, 0, -1, and a jump to the start-up code |
| `mainPos` | `mainCode`: the outermost statement `mainStmt p0`, then the verdict (`tailCode`) |
| `firstBody` | `bodiesCode`: the bodies of the procedures, each with its return sequence |
| `dispPos` | the dispatcher for the positions below `dispPos` |
| `3 * dispPos` | `startCode`: the pool, the outermost frame, and a jump back to `mainPos` |

The start-up code comes last so that no position in the rest of the code depends on its length.

The number `frameSize` of local variables of every frame is read off the text of P: it is the width
of the text.  The pool holds the numbers up to `dispPos`: every position to return to, and with them
the smaller numbers that the code needs (`two_mul_frameSize_add_le_dispPos`).

The facts proved here are about positions only: each part is where the table says
(`codeAt_headCode`, …, `codeAt_startCode`), and every body is at its entry (`codeAt_bodies`).

## References

* [AFM26] anthropics/formal-math, folder `3sum-apsp`, commit e1a4e650, Apache-2.0: the source of this file.
-/

section

open WordRAM

namespace Light.Compiler

open WordRAM (Instr)

/-! ## The number read off the text -/

/-- The number of local variables of every frame: the width of the text, and at least 3 for the
outermost frame. -/
def frameSize (P : Program) : ℕ := max 3 (maxOf Stmt.width P)

/-- The outermost frame has room for the result and the two arguments. -/
theorem three_le_frameSize (P : Program) : 3 ≤ frameSize P := le_max_left _ _

/-- Every body fits into a frame. -/
theorem width_le_frameSize {P : Program} {b : Stmt} (hb : b ∈ P) : b.width ≤ frameSize P :=
  (maxOf_le_iff.1 le_rfl b hb).trans (le_max_right _ _)

/-! ## The parts of the code -/

/-- The outermost statement: procedure p0 is called on the local variables 1 and 2 of the outermost
frame, and its result goes to local variable 0. -/
def _root_.Light.mainStmt (p0 : ℕ) : Stmt := .call p0 [.var 1, .var 2] 0

/-- What follows the outermost statement, from position pos on: the result, which is local
variable 0 of the outermost frame, goes to the cell `cRESULT`; then the machine accepts, or, if dec
is set, it accepts if the result is positive and rejects if not. -/
def tailCode (dec : Bool) (pos : ℕ) : List Instr :=
  .add cRESULT (cStack mainFrame) cZERO ::
    (if dec then [.sub cD cZERO (cStack mainFrame), .bltz cD (pos + 4), .reject, .accept]
      else [.accept])

/-- The position of the outermost statement: after the four instructions of `headCode`. -/
def mainPos : ℕ := 4

/-- The position of the code that follows the outermost statement. -/
def tailPos (F : ℕ) : ℕ := mainPos + (mainStmt 0).size F

/-- The position of the first body: after the outermost statement and what follows it. -/
def firstBody (F : ℕ) (dec : Bool) : ℕ := tailPos F + (tailCode dec 0).length

/-- The positions of the bodies, the first of them at pos.  A body is followed by the return
sequence. -/
def entries (F : ℕ) : List Stmt → ℕ → List ℕ
  | [], _ => []
  | b :: bs, pos => pos :: entries F bs (pos + b.size F + epilogueSize)

/-- The position after the bodies, the first of them at pos. -/
def endPos (F : ℕ) : List Stmt → ℕ → ℕ
  | [], pos => pos
  | b :: bs, pos => endPos F bs (pos + b.size F + epilogueSize)

/-- The position after the last body, which is the position of the dispatcher.  It is also the
largest number in the pool. -/
def dispPos (P : Program) (dec : Bool) : ℕ := endPos (frameSize P) P (firstBody (frameSize P) dec)

/-- What the code of the statements of P needs to know about the whole code. -/
def codeLayout (P : Program) (dec : Bool) : CodeLayout :=
  ⟨frameSize P, entries (frameSize P) P (firstBody (frameSize P) dec), dispPos P dec⟩

/-- The bodies with their return sequences, the first of them at pos. -/
def bodiesCode (G : CodeLayout) : List Stmt → ℕ → List Instr
  | [], _ => []
  | b :: bs, pos =>
    (compileStmt G b pos ++ epilogue G) ++ bodiesCode G bs (pos + b.size G.F + epilogueSize)

/-- The first three instructions: the numbers 1, 0, -1. -/
def headStraight : List Instr := [.one cONE, .sub cZERO cONE cONE, .sub cNEG cZERO cONE]

/-- The code that fills the pool with the numbers 0, …, N. -/
def poolCode (N : ℕ) : List Instr :=
  .sub (cPool 0) cONE cONE :: (List.range N).map fun n => .add (cPool (n + 1)) (cPool n) cONE

/-- The code that puts zeros into the local variables of the outermost frame. -/
def zeroCode (F : ℕ) : List Instr :=
  (List.range F).map fun x => .sub (cStack (mainFrame + x)) cONE cONE

/-- The code that sets up the outermost frame: FP, zeros in its local variables, and then the two
arguments in its local variables 1 and 2. -/
def frameCode (F : ℕ) : List Instr :=
  .sub cFP cZERO (cPool (2 * mainFrame)) ::
    (zeroCode F ++
      [.add (cStack (mainFrame + 1)) cARG1 cZERO, .add (cStack (mainFrame + 2)) cARG2 cZERO])

/-- The start-up code without its last jump. -/
def startStraight (F N : ℕ) : List Instr := poolCode N ++ frameCode F

/-- The first part: the numbers 1, 0, -1, and the jump to the start-up code. -/
def headCode (P : Program) (dec : Bool) : List Instr :=
  headStraight ++ [.bltz cNEG (3 * dispPos P dec)]

/-- The second part: the outermost statement and the verdict. -/
def mainCode (P : Program) (p0 : ℕ) (dec : Bool) : List Instr :=
  compileStmt (codeLayout P dec) (mainStmt p0) mainPos ++ tailCode dec (tailPos (frameSize P))

/-- The last part: the start-up code and the jump back to the outermost statement. -/
def startCode (P : Program) (dec : Bool) : List Instr :=
  startStraight (frameSize P) (dispPos P dec) ++ [.bltz cNEG mainPos]

/-- **The code of a program.**  With dec = false the machine accepts at the end of the run; with
dec = true it accepts if the result of procedure p0 is positive and rejects if not. -/
def _root_.Light.compileProgram (P : Program) (p0 : ℕ) (dec : Bool) : List Instr :=
  headCode P dec ++ mainCode P p0 dec ++ bodiesCode (codeLayout P dec) P
      (firstBody (frameSize P) dec) ++
    dispatcher (dispPos P dec) ++ startCode P dec

/-! ## Lengths and positions -/

private theorem le_endPos (F : ℕ) : ∀ (bs : List Stmt) (pos : ℕ), pos ≤ endPos F bs pos
  | [], _ => le_rfl
  | b :: bs, pos => le_trans (by omega) (le_endPos F bs (pos + b.size F + epilogueSize))

/-- The length of the outermost statement. -/
theorem size_mainStmt (p0 F : ℕ) : (mainStmt p0).size F = 2 * F + 11 := by
  simp +arith [mainStmt, Stmt.size, callSize, argsSize, Expr.size]

/-- The outermost statement uses the local variables 0, 1, 2. -/
theorem width_mainStmt (p0 : ℕ) : (mainStmt p0).width = 3 := rfl

private theorem length_tailCode (dec : Bool) (pos : ℕ) :
    (tailCode dec pos).length = if dec then 5 else 2 := by
  cases dec <;> rfl

/-- The dispatcher comes after four instructions, the 2 F + 11 instructions of the outermost
statement and at least two for the verdict.  So the pool also holds the distance 2 (F + 1) between
two frames and the number 2 · `mainFrame` from which the start-up code forms the first frame
pointer, and it reaches further down than the 2 F + 1 temporaries. -/
theorem two_mul_frameSize_add_le_dispPos (P : Program) (dec : Bool) :
    2 * frameSize P + 17 ≤ dispPos P dec := by
  have : firstBody (frameSize P) dec ≤ dispPos P dec := le_endPos _ _ _
  have htail : 2 ≤ if dec then 5 else 2 := by split_ifs <;> omega
  simp only [firstBody, tailPos, mainPos, size_mainStmt, length_tailCode] at this
  omega

private theorem length_bodiesCode (G : CodeLayout) :
    ∀ (bs : List Stmt) (pos : ℕ), pos + (bodiesCode G bs pos).length = endPos G.F bs pos
  | [], _ => rfl
  | b :: bs, pos => by
    have ih := length_bodiesCode G bs (pos + b.size G.F + epilogueSize)
    simp only [bodiesCode, endPos, List.length_append, length_compileStmt, length_epilogue]
    omega

section parts

variable (P : Program) (p0 : ℕ) (dec : Bool)

private theorem length_through_mainCode :
    (headCode P dec ++ mainCode P p0 dec).length = firstBody (frameSize P) dec := by
  simp only [headCode, headStraight, mainCode, List.length_append, length_compileStmt,
    size_mainStmt, length_tailCode, firstBody, tailPos, mainPos, codeLayout, List.length_cons,
    List.length_nil]
  omega

private theorem length_through_bodiesCode :
    (headCode P dec ++ mainCode P p0 dec ++
      bodiesCode (codeLayout P dec) P (firstBody (frameSize P) dec)).length = dispPos P dec := by
  rw [List.length_append, length_through_mainCode]
  exact length_bodiesCode (codeLayout P dec) P _

private theorem length_through_dispatcher :
    (headCode P dec ++ mainCode P p0 dec ++
      bodiesCode (codeLayout P dec) P (firstBody (frameSize P) dec) ++
      dispatcher (dispPos P dec)).length = 3 * dispPos P dec := by
  rw [List.length_append, length_through_bodiesCode, length_dispatcher]
  omega

/-- The first part is at position 0. -/
theorem codeAt_headCode : CodeAt (compileProgram P p0 dec) 0 (headCode P dec) :=
  (codeAt_self _).left.left.left.left

private theorem codeAt_mainCode : CodeAt (compileProgram P p0 dec) mainPos (mainCode P p0 dec) :=
  (codeAt_self _).left.left.left.right

/-- The outermost statement is at `mainPos`. -/
theorem codeAt_mainStmt :
    CodeAt (compileProgram P p0 dec) mainPos
      (compileStmt (codeLayout P dec) (mainStmt p0) mainPos) :=
  (codeAt_mainCode P p0 dec).left

/-- The code for the verdict follows the outermost statement. -/
theorem codeAt_tailCode :
    CodeAt (compileProgram P p0 dec) (tailPos (frameSize P))
      (tailCode dec (tailPos (frameSize P))) :=
  (codeAt_mainCode P p0 dec).right.cast_pos (by
    simp only [length_compileStmt, size_mainStmt, tailPos, codeLayout])

/-- The bodies begin at `firstBody`. -/
theorem codeAt_bodiesCode :
    CodeAt (compileProgram P p0 dec) (firstBody (frameSize P) dec)
      (bodiesCode (codeLayout P dec) P (firstBody (frameSize P) dec)) :=
  (codeAt_self _).left.left.right.cast_pos (by rw [length_through_mainCode, Nat.zero_add])

/-- The dispatcher is at `dispPos`. -/
theorem codeAt_dispatcher_dispPos :
    CodeAt (compileProgram P p0 dec) (dispPos P dec) (dispatcher (dispPos P dec)) :=
  (codeAt_self _).left.right.cast_pos (by rw [length_through_bodiesCode, Nat.zero_add])

/-- The start-up code follows the dispatcher. -/
theorem codeAt_startCode :
    CodeAt (compileProgram P p0 dec) (3 * dispPos P dec) (startCode P dec) :=
  (codeAt_self _).right.cast_pos (by rw [length_through_dispatcher, Nat.zero_add])

end parts

/-- Every body is at its entry, followed by the return sequence, and ends at or before the position
after the bodies. -/
theorem codeAt_bodies {code : List Instr} (G : CodeLayout) :
    ∀ (bs : List Stmt) (pos : ℕ), CodeAt code pos (bodiesCode G bs pos) →
      ∀ (p : ℕ) (b : Stmt), bs[p]? = some b → ∃ e, (entries G.F bs pos)[p]? = some e ∧
        CodeAt code e (compileStmt G b e ++ epilogue G) ∧
        e + b.size G.F + epilogueSize ≤ endPos G.F bs pos
  | [], _, _, p, b, h => by simp at h
  | b₀ :: bs, pos, hc, 0, b, h => by
    obtain rfl : b₀ = b := by simpa using h
    exact ⟨pos, rfl, hc.left, le_endPos _ _ _⟩
  | b₀ :: bs, pos, hc, p + 1, b, h =>
    codeAt_bodies G bs _
      (hc.right.cast_pos (by
        rw [List.length_append, length_compileStmt, length_epilogue, Nat.add_assoc])) p b
      (by simpa using h)

end Light.Compiler
end
```

## ===== TCSlib/Programs/Light/Compiler/WholeProgram.lean =====

```
/-
Copyright (c) 2026 Anthropic, PBC. All rights reserved.
Released under Apache 2.0 license as described in the file LICENSE.
SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0

Vendored from [AFM26] anthropics/formal-math (https://github.com/anthropics/formal-math), folder
`3sum-apsp`, file `ThreeSumApsp/Lang/Compiler/WholeProgram.lean`, at commit e1a4e6508154ea59f030480661590a9fe3018011 (2026-10-05).
Local modifications (see policy.md §2, vendored code, and the repository NOTICE):
* removed the Lean module-system syntax (`module`, `public import`, `@[expose] public section`)
  for compatibility with our v4.25.0 toolchain;
* moved to `TCSlib/Programs/Light/Compiler/WholeProgram.lean`, with imports remapped;
* namespaces `EndStatement` and `ThreeSumApsp.WordRam` renamed to `WordRAM`, and `ThreeSumApsp` to `Light`, with module names and paths in comments updated;
* added a `## References` section citing [AFM26];
* added the repository-standard `set_option` header.
The remaining content is unchanged.
-/
import TCSlib.Programs.Light.Compiler.Simulation
import TCSlib.Programs.Light.Compiler.StartUp

set_option maxHeartbeats 0
set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
set_option autoImplicit false

/-!
# The compiler is correct on whole programs

`compileProgram_correct`: if the call of procedure p0 of the light program P ends after c
steps, the compiled program gives its verdict within `ramSteps P dec c` = c₀ + K c steps, where c₀
and K depend on the text of P (and on dec) only.  If the memory of the machine held the memory of
the light program and the two arguments before (`Input`), it holds the final memory afterwards, with
the result in the cell -3 (`Outcome`).

The run of the machine has three parts.

1. The start-up code (`steps_start`) leads to a memory that represents the first state
   (`rel_startMem`).
2. The outermost statement is the call of p0.  The compiled program satisfies what the simulation
   theorem assumes (`setting`, `start_mainStmt`), so `sim` gives a run of at most K c steps to a
   memory that represents the last state.
3. The code that follows copies the result to the cell -3 and gives the verdict (`steps_tail`).

Each part comes with the set of cells that it may change.  The cells below `-lowCell` and the cells
from `lim.space` on are in none of the three sets (`not_mem_of_far`), and neither are the cells -1
and -2.

The notions of the compiler and of its proof are in the namespace `Light.Compiler`.  The statements
about programs name four of them, which are in `Light`: `compileProgram`, `mainStmt`, `verdictOf`,
`ramSteps`.  The passage to the notions of the word RAM also uses `compileProgram_correct` with
`Fits`, `Holds`, `Input` and `Outcome`, the cells `cARG1`, `cARG2`, `cRESULT`, and the numbers
`startCost`, `stepsPerStep`, `dispPos`, `frameSize`, `stackCells`, `lowCell`.

## References

* [AFM26] anthropics/formal-math, folder `3sum-apsp`, commit e1a4e650, Apache-2.0: the source of this file.
-/

section

open WordRAM

namespace Light.Compiler

open WordRAM (Instr exec)

variable {W : ℕ}

/-! ## The verdict -/

/-- The verdict: accept, or, if dec is set, accept exactly if the result is positive. -/
def _root_.Light.verdictOf (dec : Bool) (r : ℤ) : Bool := !dec || decide (0 < r)

/-- What the code that follows the outermost statement leaves, if it starts in the memory m and the
result is r: the machine is about to give the verdict, the cell `cRESULT` holds the result, and only
that cell and the register D have changed. -/
private structure Verdict (code : List Instr) (dec : Bool) (r : ℤ) (m : ℤ → BitVec W)
    (cfg : Cfg W) : Prop where
  verdict : step code cfg = .inr (verdictOf dec r)
  result : cfg.mem cRESULT = wd W r
  agree : AgreeOutside {cRESULT, cD} m cfg.mem

/-- The code that follows the outermost statement gets there in at most three steps. -/
private theorem steps_tail {code : List Instr} {pos : ℕ} {dec : Bool}
    (hcode : CodeAt code pos (tailCode dec pos)) {m : ℤ → BitVec W} {r : ℤ}
    (hres : m (cStack mainFrame) = wd W r) (hzero : m cZERO = wd W 0) (hr : InRange W (-r)) :
    ∃ (n : ℕ) (cfg : Cfg W), n ≤ 3 ∧ Steps code n ⟨pos, m⟩ cfg ∧ Verdict code dec r m cfg := by
  -- the cell for the result := the result
  have copy : Steps code 1 ⟨pos, m⟩ ⟨pos + 1, Function.update m cRESULT (wd W r)⟩ :=
    add_zero r ▸ steps_add hcode hres hzero
  cases dec with
  | false =>
    -- accept
    have read : Function.update m cRESULT (wd W r) cRESULT = wd W r := Function.update_self ..
    exact ⟨1, _, by omega, copy, step_accept hcode.tail.head, read,
      (AgreeOutside.refl _ m).update (by simp) _⟩
  | true =>
    have rest : CodeAt code (pos + 1)
      [.sub cD cZERO (cStack mainFrame), .bltz cD (pos + 4), .reject, .accept] := hcode.tail
    -- D := 0 - the result; go to accept if D < 0; reject
    have run := (copy.trans (steps_sub rest (by cell_read using hzero)
      (by cell_read using hres))).trans
      (steps_bltz rest.tail (Function.update_self ..) (by rwa [zero_sub]))
    have keep : AgreeOutside {cRESULT, cD} m
        (Function.update (Function.update m cRESULT (wd W r)) cD (wd W (0 - r))) :=
      ((AgreeOutside.refl _ m).update (by simp) _).update (by simp) _
    have read : Function.update (Function.update m cRESULT (wd W r)) cD (wd W (0 - r)) cRESULT =
        wd W r := by
      cell_read
    by_cases hpos : 0 < r
    · rw [if_pos (by omega)] at run
      exact ⟨3, _, le_rfl, run,
        (step_accept rest.tail.tail.tail.head).trans (by simp [verdictOf, hpos]), read, keep⟩
    · rw [if_neg (by omega)] at run
      exact ⟨3, _, le_rfl, run,
        (step_reject rest.tail.tail.head).trans (by simp [verdictOf, hpos]), read, keep⟩

/-! ## Time and space of the compiled program -/

/-- A bound for the number of steps of the compiled program outside the outermost statement:
`dispPos` + `frameSize` + 9 before it (`steps_start`), at most three after it (`steps_tail`), and
the verdict. -/
def startCost (P : Program) (dec : Bool) : ℕ := dispPos P dec + frameSize P + 13

/-- A bound for the number of steps of the compiled program, for c steps of the light program. -/
def _root_.Light.ramSteps (P : Program) (dec : Bool) (c : ℕ) : ℕ :=
  startCost P dec + stepsPerStep (dispPos P dec) * c

/-- The number of cells of the stack that a run within the limits needs. -/
def stackCells (P : Program) (lim : Limits) : ℕ :=
  mainFrame + (frameSize P + 1) * (lim.depth + 1)

/-- A bound for the extent of the negative cells that the compiled program uses: the stack ends
before the cell -2 · stackCells, and the pool, which reaches further down than the temporaries, ends
at the cell -(25 + 4 · dispPos). -/
def lowCell (P : Program) (dec : Bool) (lim : Limits) : ℕ :=
  max (2 * stackCells P lim) (25 + 4 * dispPos P dec)

private theorem frame_le_stackCells (P : Program) (lim : Limits) :
    mainFrame + frameSize P + 1 ≤ stackCells P lim := by
  have := Nat.le_mul_of_pos_right (frameSize P + 1) (show 0 < lim.depth + 1 by omega)
  unfold stackCells
  omega

/-! ## The setting of the simulation theorem -/

section setting

variable {lim : Limits} {P : Program} {p0 : ℕ} {dec : Bool}

/-- The compiled program, with its numbers, satisfies what the simulation theorem assumes once and
for all. -/
private def setting (P : Program) (p0 : ℕ) (dec : Bool)
    (hfit : Fits W lim (dispPos P dec) (stackCells P lim)) : Setting W where
  toCodeLayout := codeLayout P dec
  lim := lim
  Q := stackCells P lim
  fits := hfit
  offsets_le := by
    have := two_mul_frameSize_add_le_dispPos P dec
    simp only [codeLayout]
    omega
  P := P
  code := compileProgram P p0 dec
  bodyAt := codeAt_bodies (codeLayout P dec) P _ (codeAt_bodiesCode P p0 dec)
  dispatcherAt := codeAt_dispatcher_dispPos P p0 dec
  width _ hb := width_le_frameSize hb

variable (hfit : Fits W lim (dispPos P dec) (stackCells P lim))

/-- The outermost statement is at `mainPos` and runs in the frame `mainFrame`. -/
private theorem start_mainStmt {σ : State} {m : ℤ → BitVec W} (hσ : σ.Bounded lim)
    (R : Rel (setting P p0 dec hfit).toSizes σ mainFrame m) :
    Start (setting P p0 dec hfit) 0 (mainStmt p0) σ mainPos mainFrame m where
  rel := R
  low := by decide
  high := (Nat.le_succ _).trans (frame_le_stackCells P lim)
  codeAt := codeAt_mainStmt P p0 dec
  below := by
    have := two_mul_frameSize_add_le_dispPos P dec
    simp only [size_mainStmt, setting, codeLayout, mainPos]
    omega
  width := (width_mainStmt p0).trans_le (three_le_frameSize P)
  bounded := hσ
  room := by
    simp only [setting, codeLayout, stackCells, Nat.sub_zero, Nat.mul_succ]
    omega

/-- The cells below `-lowCell` and the cells from `lim.space` on are not among those that the
start-up code, the outermost statement or the code for the verdict may change. -/
private theorem not_mem_of_far {a : ℤ}
    (ha : a < -(lowCell P dec lim : ℤ) ∨ (lim.space : ℤ) ≤ a) :
    a ∉ Constants (dispPos P dec) ∪ ({cFP} ∪ MainFrame (frameSize P)) ∪
      Writable (setting P p0 dec hfit).toSizes mainFrame ∪ {cRESULT, cD} := by
  have hframe := frame_le_stackCells P lim
  have htemps := two_mul_frameSize_add_le_dispPos P dec
  have hstack : 2 * stackCells P lim ≤ lowCell P dec lim := le_max_left _ _
  have hpool : 25 + 4 * dispPos P dec ≤ lowCell P dec lim := le_max_right _ _
  simp only [Writable, setting, codeLayout]
  rcases ha with ha | ha <;> cells

end setting

/-! ## The theorem -/

/-- The cells 0, 1, 2, … of the memory m of the machine hold the memory μ of the light language,
and μ holds words. -/
structure Holds (W : ℕ) (lim : Limits) (μ : ℕ → ℤ) (m : ℤ → BitVec W) : Prop where
  rep : ∀ a : ℕ, m (a : ℤ) = wd W (μ a)
  bounded : ∀ a, |μ a| ≤ lim.word

/-- What a run of a compiled program needs in the memory m: the memory μ of the light language, and
the arguments i and j, which are words, in their two cells. -/
structure Input (lim : Limits) (μ : ℕ → ℤ) (i j : ℤ) (m : ℤ → BitVec W) : Prop
    extends Holds W lim μ m where
  arg1 : m cARG1 = wd W i
  arg2 : m cARG2 = wd W j
  arg1_le : |i| ≤ lim.word
  arg2_le : |j| ≤ lim.word

/-- What a run of a compiled program from the memory m leaves in the memory m', if the light run
ends in σ'. -/
structure Outcome (lim : Limits) (P : Program) (dec : Bool) (m : ℤ → BitVec W) (σ' : State)
    (m' : ℤ → BitVec W) : Prop where
  /-- The cells 0, 1, 2, … hold the final memory. -/
  holds : Holds W lim σ'.mem m'
  /-- The cell for the result holds the result. -/
  result : (m' cRESULT).toInt = σ'.loc 0
  /-- The first argument is still there. -/
  arg1 : m' cARG1 = m cARG1
  /-- The second argument is still there. -/
  arg2 : m' cARG2 = m cARG2
  /-- The cells far below 0 and the cells from lim.space on are unchanged. -/
  far : ∀ a : ℤ, a < -(lowCell P dec lim : ℤ) ∨ (lim.space : ℤ) ≤ a → m' a = m a

/-- **The compiler is correct.**  Let the call of procedure p0 of the program P on the arguments i,
j and the memory μ end after c steps within the limits, and let the word size fit the limits.  Then
the compiled program, started on any memory whose cells `a ≥ 0` hold the words of μ and whose
cells -1 and -2 hold i and j, gives its verdict within `startCost + stepsPerStep · c` steps.  Then
the cell -3 holds the result of the procedure and the cells `a ≥ 0` hold the final memory.  The
cells -1 and -2, the cells below `-lowCell` and the cells from `lim.space` on are unchanged.  The
two constants of the time depend on the text of P and on dec only. -/
theorem compileProgram_correct {lim : Limits} {P : Program} {p0 : ℕ} {dec : Bool} {μ : ℕ → ℤ}
    {i j : ℤ} {σ' : State} {c : ℕ} {m : ℤ → BitVec W}
    (h : Exec lim P 0 (mainStmt p0) ⟨frame [0, i, j], μ⟩ σ' c)
    (hfit : Fits W lim (dispPos P dec) (stackCells P lim)) (hm : Input lim μ i j m) :
    ∃ m' : ℤ → BitVec W, exec (compileProgram P p0 dec) (ramSteps P dec c) 0 m
        = some (verdictOf dec (σ'.loc 0), m') ∧ Outcome lim P dec m σ' m' := by
  have hF := three_le_frameSize P
  have hdisp := two_mul_frameSize_add_le_dispPos P dec
  have hσ : State.Bounded lim ⟨frame [0, i, j], μ⟩ :=
    bounded_frame (by simp [hm.arg1_le, hm.arg2_le, (abs_nonneg i).trans hm.arg1_le]) hm.bounded
  have hσ' := h.bounded hσ
  -- the start-up code
  have run₁ := steps_start P p0 dec (setting P p0 dec hfit).inRange_neg_one m
  have keep₁ := agreeOutside_startMem hF (dispPos P dec) m
  -- the outermost statement
  obtain ⟨m', n, fin⟩ := sim (setting P p0 dec hfit) h mainPos mainFrame _ (start_mainStmt hfit hσ
    (rel_startMem _ hF (by simp only [setting, codeLayout, mainFrame]; omega)
      (fun a _ => hm.rep a) hm.arg1 hm.arg2))
  have hn : n ≤ stepsPerStep (dispPos P dec) * c := fin.le
  have run₂ := fin.run.cast_pos (p' := tailPos (frameSize P))
    (by simp only [tailPos, size_mainStmt, setting, codeLayout])
  -- the verdict
  obtain ⟨k, cfg, hk, run₃, tail⟩ := steps_tail (codeAt_tailCode P p0 dec)
    (fin.rel.loc 0 (show 0 < frameSize P by omega)) fin.rel.zero
    (hfit.inRange (by rw [abs_neg]; exact hσ'.1 0))
  have run := (run₁.trans run₂).trans run₃
  have keep := (keep₁.trans_union fin.agree).trans_union tail.agree
  have far (a : ℤ) ha : cfg.mem a = m a := keep.cell (not_mem_of_far hfit ha)
  refine ⟨cfg.mem, exec_mono (c := ⟨0, m⟩) (exec_of_steps run tail.verdict) ?_,
    { holds := ⟨fun a => ?_, hσ'.2⟩
      result := tail.result ▸ toInt_wd (hfit.inRange (hσ'.1 0))
      arg1 := keep.cell (by cells)
      arg2 := keep.cell (by cells)
      far := far }⟩
  · simp only [ramSteps, startCost]
    omega
  · by_cases ha : a < lim.space
    · exact tail.agree.read (by cells) (fin.rel.mem a ha)
    · rw [far _ (Or.inr (by exact_mod_cast not_lt.1 ha)), h.mem_outside a (not_lt.1 ha)]
      exact hm.rep a

end Light.Compiler
end
```

## ===== TCSlib/Programs/WordRAM/Extended.lean =====

```
/-
Copyright (c) 2026 Seyoon Ragavan. All rights reserved.
Released under Apache 2.0 license as described in the file LICENSE.
Authors: Seyoon Ragavan
-/
import TCSlib.Programs.WordRAM.Steps
import Mathlib.Data.Finset.Card

set_option maxHeartbeats 0
set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
set_option autoImplicit false

/-!
# The extended word RAM: input, output, coins and halving; footprints and halting

The word RAM of [AFM26] (`WordRAM.Instr`, nine instructions) is extended by a wrapper:
`WordRAM.XInstr` has the vendored instructions as `XInstr.base`, and four more:

* `rdIn i j`, the input bit at the position held in a cell, read in place;
* `emit i`, which appends the bit "`[i]` is nonzero" to an append-only output;
* `coin i`, the next coin of a read-once coin stream;
* `half i j`, an arithmetic shift right.

A configuration `WordRAM.XCfg` extends `WordRAM.Cfg` by the emitted bits and the number of
coins consumed. Input and coin streams are parameters of a run. The vendored run calculus
applies through `XProgram.proj`, as proved by `XSteps.of_steps_proj`.

The run calculus also records which memory cells a run touches and when the program halts
within given bounds on steps and cells. All run, footprint, and halting results below are proved.

## Main definitions

* `WordRAM.XInstr`, `WordRAM.XProgram`, `WordRAM.XCfg`, `WordRAM.xstep`, `WordRAM.XSteps`:
  the extended machine and its runs.
* `WordRAM.CoinFree`, `WordRAM.XProgram.proj`: coin-free programs and the vendored projection.
* `WordRAM.baseTouches`, `WordRAM.XInstr.touches`: the cells one instruction reads or writes.
* `WordRAM.StepsIn`, `WordRAM.XStepsIn`: runs within a footprint.
* `WordRAM.initMem`, `WordRAM.XCfg.init`, `WordRAM.HaltsWithin`: initialization and bounded halting.

## Main results

* `WordRAM.XSteps.trans`, `WordRAM.xstep_map_base`, `WordRAM.XSteps.of_steps`,
  `WordRAM.XSteps.of_steps_proj`: composition and conservativity over the vendored RAM.
* `WordRAM.StepsIn.agreeOutside`: intermediate memories agree outside the footprint.
* `WordRAM.stepsIn_straight`: straight-line execution with a footprint.
* `WordRAM.XSteps.exists_xStepsIn`, `WordRAM.HaltsWithin.touches_le`: a run of `n` steps
  touches at most `3 * n` cells, giving a cell bound from a time bound.
* `WordRAM.HaltsWithin.mono`, `WordRAM.HaltsWithin.unique`, `WordRAM.HaltsWithin.coin_irrel`:
  monotonicity, determinism, and independence of the coin stream for coin-free programs.
* Readers (equations of the definitions, for other files): `WordRAM.inBitWord_def`,
  `WordRAM.coinWord_def`, `WordRAM.xstep_def`, `WordRAM.XSteps.zero_iff`, `.succ_iff`,
  `WordRAM.baseTouches_def`, `WordRAM.XInstr.touches_def`, `WordRAM.XStepsIn.zero_iff`,
  `.succ_iff`, `WordRAM.initMem_def`, `WordRAM.XCfg.init_def`, `WordRAM.haltsWithin_iff`.

## References

* [AFM26] Anthropic, PBC, `anthropics/formal-math` (https://github.com/anthropics/formal-math),
  project `3sum-apsp`, commit `e1a4e6508154ea59f030480661590a9fe3018011` (2026-10-05),
  Apache-2.0, © Anthropic, PBC. Design adaptation: the word RAM of `EndStatement.lean`
  and the run calculus of `ThreeSumApsp/Machine/Steps.lean`, vendored as
  `TCSlib/Programs/WordRAM/Basic.lean` and `Steps.lean`, which this file wraps. `xstep` restates
  the match of `WordRAM.step`, routing the six memory instructions through `WordRAM.effect`;
  `XSteps` and `StepsIn` use the same finite-run convention as `WordRAM.Steps`. The proofs cite
  the vendored equations and lemmas. The footprint notion is ours: upstream's
  `ThreeSumApsp/Machine/Footprint.lean` bounds how many cells a run changes and reads, not which.
-/

namespace WordRAM

/-! ## The machine -/

/-- **The instructions of the extended word RAM** (design §15c, §15d): the nine instructions of
the vendored `WordRAM.Instr`, as `base`, and four more. `rdIn i j` sets `[i]` to the input bit at
position `[j]`, as `0` or `1` (`0` off the input, negative positions included); `emit i` appends
the bit "`[i]` is nonzero" to the output; `coin i` sets `[i]` to the next coin, as `0` or `1`,
and consumes it; `half i j` sets `[i]` to `[j]` shifted right by one place with the sign kept,
that is `⌊[j] / 2⌋`. The extension wraps `Instr` instead of adding constructors to it (user
decision, 2026-10-12), so the vendored files and their run calculus are unchanged.
[AFM26, `EndStatement.lean`], extended per design §15c. -/
inductive XInstr : Type where
  | base (i : Instr)
  | rdIn (i j : ℤ)
  | emit (i : ℤ)
  | coin (i : ℤ)
  | half (i j : ℤ)

/-- A program of the extended word RAM: its instructions, at positions `0, 1, 2, …`. -/
abbrev XProgram : Type := List XInstr

/-- **A configuration of the extended RAM**: the vendored configuration (the position of the
next instruction and the memory), the bits emitted so far, and the number of coins consumed so
far. The input and the coin stream are not part of it: they are parameters of a run. -/
structure XCfg (W : ℕ) extends Cfg W where
  /-- The bits emitted so far, oldest first. -/
  out : List Bool
  /-- The number of coins consumed so far. -/
  rd : ℕ

/-- The word that `rdIn` writes for the position `p` of the input `x`: `1` if `0 ≤ p` and the bit
of `x` at `p` is `true`, and `0` otherwise, in particular off the input. -/
def inBitWord (W : ℕ) (x : List Bool) (p : ℤ) : BitVec W :=
  if 0 ≤ p ∧ x.getD p.toNat false = true then 1 else 0

/-- The word that `coin` writes for the coin `b`: `1` for `true`, `0` for `false`. -/
def coinWord (W : ℕ) (b : Bool) : BitVec W := if b then 1 else 0

/-- **One step of the extended RAM** on the input `x` with the coin stream `ρ`: the next
configuration, or the verdict. The nine vendored instructions behave as in `WordRAM.step` (the
six that only change the memory through the vendored `WordRAM.effect`) and leave the output and
the coin count alone; past its end the program rejects. The coin is read as `ρ.getD rd false`,
so a stream that has run out reads `false`; quantifying over all lists `ρ` covers every stream. -/
def xstep {W : ℕ} (P : XProgram) (x ρ : List Bool) (c : XCfg W) : XCfg W ⊕ Bool :=
  let m := c.mem
  match P.getD c.pc (.base .reject) with
  | .base .accept => .inr true
  | .base .reject => .inr false
  | .base (.bltz i l) => .inl { c with pc := if (m i).toInt < 0 then l else c.pc + 1 }
  | .base ins => .inl { c with pc := c.pc + 1, mem := effect ins m }
  | .rdIn i j =>
    .inl { c with pc := c.pc + 1, mem := Function.update m i (inBitWord W x (m j).toInt) }
  | .emit i => .inl { c with pc := c.pc + 1, out := c.out ++ [decide (m i ≠ 0)] }
  | .coin i =>
    .inl { c with pc := c.pc + 1, mem := Function.update m i (coinWord W (ρ.getD c.rd false)),
                  rd := c.rd + 1 }
  | .half i j => .inl { c with pc := c.pc + 1, mem := Function.update m i ((m j).sshiftRight 1) }

/-- `XSteps P x ρ n c c'`: exactly `n` steps of `P` on the input `x` with the coin stream `ρ`
lead from `c` to `c'`, none of them a verdict. The extended counterpart of `WordRAM.Steps`. -/
def XSteps {W : ℕ} (P : XProgram) (x ρ : List Bool) : ℕ → XCfg W → XCfg W → Prop
  | 0, c, c' => c = c'
  | n + 1, c, c' => ∃ c₁, xstep P x ρ c = .inl c₁ ∧ XSteps P x ρ n c₁ c'

/-- A program is **coin-free** when none of its instructions is a `coin`. -/
def CoinFree (P : XProgram) : Prop := ∀ ins ∈ P, ∀ a : ℤ, ins ≠ .coin a

/-- **The vendored projection** of a program of the extended RAM: the same program with each new
instruction replaced by `reject`. A run of the projection that gives no verdict never executes a
replaced position, so it is also a run of the extended program (`XSteps.of_steps_proj`). This is
how the vendored run calculus and the compiler's segment lemmas serve code that mixes old and new
instructions. -/
def XProgram.proj (P : XProgram) : List Instr :=
  P.map fun
    | .base i => i
    | _ => .reject

/-! ## Private instruction comparison -/

/-- Mapping a fetch follows `List.getElem?_map` and `Option.getD_map`. -/
private theorem mapped_fetch {α β : Type} (l : List α) (d : α) (f : α → β) (n : ℕ) :
    (l.map f).getD n (f d) = f (l.getD n d) := by
  simp only [List.getD_eq_getElem?_getD, List.getElem?_map, Option.getD_map]

/-- A base instruction has the same effect in both machines. The instruction cases use
`step_def` and `effect_def`; `Function.update_apply` identifies the memory functions. -/
private theorem xstep_base_fetch {W : ℕ} {P : XProgram} {Q : List Instr}
    (x ρ : List Bool) (c : Cfg W) (o : List Bool) (r : ℕ)
    (h : P.getD c.pc (.base .reject) = .base (Q.getD c.pc .reject)) :
    xstep P x ρ ⟨c, o, r⟩ =
      Sum.map (fun d => (⟨d, o, r⟩ : XCfg W)) id (step Q c) := by
  rw [xstep, h, step_def]
  cases Q.getD c.pc .reject <;>
    simp only [effect_def, Sum.map_inl, Sum.map_inr, id_eq]
  all_goals congr 3; funext a; simp only [Function.update_apply]; try rfl

/-- A non-verdict step of the projection can only fetch a base instruction. -/
private theorem projected_fetch {W : ℕ} {P : XProgram} {c d : Cfg W}
    (hs : step P.proj c = .inl d) :
    P.getD c.pc (.base .reject) = .base (P.proj.getD c.pc .reject) := by
  have hget : P.proj.getD c.pc .reject =
      (match P.getD c.pc (.base .reject) with | .base i => i | _ => .reject) :=
    by simpa only [XProgram.proj] using
      (mapped_fetch (β := Instr) P (.base .reject)
        (fun i : XInstr => match i with | .base j => j | _ => Instr.reject) c.pc)
  cases hi : P.getD c.pc (.base .reject) <;> simp only [hi] at hget
  case base i => exact congrArg XInstr.base hget.symm
  all_goals
    have hv := step_reject hget
    rw [hs] at hv
    contradiction

/-! ## Private run lifting -/

/-- A map that preserves single steps preserves finite runs. -/
private theorem lift_run {C D : Type} {S : C → C → Prop} {T : D → D → Prop}
    {R : ℕ → C → C → Prop} {R' : ℕ → D → D → Prop}
    (hz : ∀ c d, R 0 c d ↔ c = d)
    (hs : ∀ n c d, R (n + 1) c d ↔ ∃ e, S c e ∧ R n e d)
    (hz' : ∀ c d, R' 0 c d ↔ c = d)
    (hs' : ∀ n c d, R' (n + 1) c d ↔ ∃ e, T c e ∧ R' n e d)
    (f : C → D) (hstep : ∀ c d, S c d → T (f c) (f d))
    {n : ℕ} {c d : C} (h : R n c d) : R' n (f c) (f d) := by
  induction n generalizing c with
  | zero => exact (hz' _ _).mpr (congrArg f ((hz c d).mp h))
  | succ n ih =>
    obtain ⟨e, hstep', hr⟩ := (hs n c d).mp h
    exact (hs' n _ _).mpr ⟨f e, hstep c e hstep', ih hr⟩

section RunCalculus

variable {W : ℕ} {P : XProgram} {x ρ : List Bool}

/-- One run after the other: `n` steps from `c` to `c₁`, then `k` steps from `c₁` to `c₂`, are
`n + k` steps from `c` to `c₂`. The counterpart of `WordRAM.Steps.trans`.

**Proof sketch.** Induct on the first run length, generalizing the starting configuration.
The zero case is the second run; the successor case keeps the first step and composes the
tail by induction, as in `WordRAM.Steps.trans`. -/
theorem XSteps.trans {n k : ℕ} {c c₁ c₂ : XCfg W} (h₁ : XSteps P x ρ n c c₁)
    (h₂ : XSteps P x ρ k c₁ c₂) : XSteps P x ρ (n + k) c c₂ := by
  induction n generalizing c with
  | zero =>
    obtain rfl := h₁
    simpa only [Nat.zero_add] using h₂
  | succ n ih =>
    obtain ⟨d, hs, hr⟩ := h₁
    rw [Nat.add_right_comm]
    exact ⟨d, hs, ih hr⟩

/-- **Conservativity, one step.** On a program of vendored instructions, a step of the extended
RAM is the vendored step, with the output and the coin count unchanged.

**Proof sketch.** `mapped_fetch` identifies the instruction in the mapped program.
Apply `xstep_base_fetch`, whose single instruction case analysis uses `step_def` and
`effect_def`; `Function.update_apply` identifies the memory functions. -/
theorem xstep_map_base (P : List Instr) (c : Cfg W) (o : List Bool) (r : ℕ) :
    xstep (P.map .base) x ρ ⟨c, o, r⟩ =
      Sum.map (fun c' => (⟨c', o, r⟩ : XCfg W)) id (step P c) := by
  exact xstep_base_fetch x ρ c o r (mapped_fetch P .reject XInstr.base c.pc)

/-- **Conservativity, runs.** A run of a vendored program is a run of its extended copy, with
the output and the coin count unchanged.

**Proof sketch.** Apply `lift_run` with the configuration embedding that preserves
the output and coin count. `Steps.zero_iff` and `Steps.succ_iff` expose the vendored run,
and `xstep_map_base` preserves each step. -/
theorem XSteps.of_steps {P : List Instr} {n : ℕ} {c c' : Cfg W} (h : Steps P n c c')
    (o : List Bool) (r : ℕ) : XSteps (P.map .base) x ρ n ⟨c, o, r⟩ ⟨c', o, r⟩ := by
  refine lift_run (S := fun c d => step P c = .inl d)
    (T := fun c d => xstep (P.map .base) x ρ c = .inl d)
    (fun _ _ => Steps.zero_iff) (fun _ _ _ => Steps.succ_iff)
    (fun _ _ => Iff.rfl) (fun _ _ _ => Iff.rfl)
    (fun d => (⟨d, o, r⟩ : XCfg W)) (fun c d hs => ?_) h
  change xstep (P.map .base) x ρ ⟨c, o, r⟩ = .inl ⟨d, o, r⟩
  rw [xstep_map_base, hs, Sum.map_inl]

/-- **The vendored run calculus on mixed code.** A verdict-free run of the projection `P.proj` is
a run of `P` itself, with the output and the coin count unchanged.

**Proof sketch.** Use `lift_run` with the configuration embedding. At each step,
`projected_fetch` rules out a replacement instruction using `step_reject`.
`xstep_base_fetch` then identifies the extended transition with the projected one. -/
theorem XSteps.of_steps_proj {n : ℕ} {c c' : Cfg W} (h : Steps P.proj n c c')
    (o : List Bool) (r : ℕ) : XSteps P x ρ n ⟨c, o, r⟩ ⟨c', o, r⟩ := by
  refine lift_run (S := fun c d => step P.proj c = .inl d)
    (T := fun c d => xstep P x ρ c = .inl d)
    (fun _ _ => Steps.zero_iff) (fun _ _ _ => Steps.succ_iff)
    (fun _ _ => Iff.rfl) (fun _ _ _ => Iff.rfl)
    (fun d => (⟨d, o, r⟩ : XCfg W)) (fun c d hs => ?_) h
  change xstep P x ρ ⟨c, o, r⟩ = .inl ⟨d, o, r⟩
  rw [xstep_base_fetch x ρ c o r (projected_fetch hs), hs, Sum.map_inl]

end RunCalculus

/-! ## The cells a run touches (backlog PROG-3, RAM level) -/

/-- The cells that a vendored instruction reads or writes in the memory `m`: the cells it names
and, for `load` and `store`, the cell whose address it reads from memory (the source of `load`,
the target of `store`). The verdicts touch nothing. -/
def baseTouches {W : ℕ} (ins : Instr) (m : ℤ → BitVec W) : Finset ℤ :=
  match ins with
  | .one i => {i}
  | .add i j k => {i, j, k}
  | .sub i j k => {i, j, k}
  | .mul i j k => {i, j, k}
  | .load i j => {i, j, (m j).toInt}
  | .store i j => {i, j, (m i).toInt}
  | .bltz i _ => {i}
  | .accept => ∅
  | .reject => ∅

/-- The cells that an instruction of the extended RAM reads or writes in the memory `m`: those of
`baseTouches` for a vendored instruction, and the cells named by a new one. The input and the
coin stream are not cells. -/
def XInstr.touches {W : ℕ} : XInstr → (ℤ → BitVec W) → Finset ℤ
  | .base ins, m => baseTouches ins m
  | .rdIn i j, _ => {i, j}
  | .emit i, _ => {i}
  | .coin i, _ => {i}
  | .half i j, _ => {i, j}

/-- Every instruction touches at most three cells.

**Proof sketch.** Case on the instruction and use `Finset.card_le_three`,
`Finset.card_le_two`, `Finset.card_singleton`, and `Finset.card_empty` for the listed cells. -/
theorem XInstr.card_touches_le {W : ℕ} (ins : XInstr) (m : ℤ → BitVec W) :
    (ins.touches m).card ≤ 3 := by
  cases ins <;> simp only [touches]
  case base i =>
    cases i <;> simp only [baseTouches] <;>
      first | exact Finset.card_le_three
            | simp only [Finset.card_singleton, Finset.card_empty]; omega
  all_goals first
    | exact Finset.card_le_two.trans (by decide)
    | simp only [Finset.card_singleton]; omega

/-- `XStepsIn P x ρ K n c c'`: an `XSteps` run in which every step reads and writes only cells of
`K`, the *footprint*. Every run is such a run for the set of the cells it touches
(`XSteps.exists_xStepsIn`). -/
def XStepsIn {W : ℕ} (P : XProgram) (x ρ : List Bool) (K : Set ℤ) :
    ℕ → XCfg W → XCfg W → Prop
  | 0, c, c' => c = c'
  | n + 1, c, c' => ∃ c₁, xstep P x ρ c = .inl c₁ ∧
      ↑((P.getD c.pc (.base .reject)).touches c.mem) ⊆ K ∧ XStepsIn P x ρ K n c₁ c'

/-- `StepsIn P K n c c'`: a `WordRAM.Steps` run of a vendored program in which every step reads
and writes only cells of `K`. The PROG-3 strengthening of `WordRAM.Steps`, added beside it; the
vendored definition is unchanged. -/
def StepsIn {W : ℕ} (P : List Instr) (K : Set ℤ) : ℕ → Cfg W → Cfg W → Prop
  | 0, c, c' => c = c'
  | n + 1, c, c' => ∃ c₁, step P c = .inl c₁ ∧
      ↑(baseTouches (P.getD c.pc .reject) c.mem) ⊆ K ∧ StepsIn P K n c₁ c'

/-! ## Private footprint inclusion -/

/-- Footprint inclusion for any run relation with the same first-step equations. -/
private theorem footprint_mono {C : Type} {S : C → C → Prop} {F : C → Finset ℤ}
    {R : Set ℤ → ℕ → C → C → Prop}
    (hz : ∀ K c d, R K 0 c d ↔ c = d)
    (hs : ∀ K n c d, R K (n + 1) c d ↔
      ∃ e, S c e ∧ ↑(F c) ⊆ K ∧ R K n e d)
    {K L : Set ℤ} {n : ℕ} {c d : C} (h : R K n c d) (hKL : K ⊆ L) :
    R L n c d := by
  induction n generalizing c with
  | zero => exact (hz L c d).mpr ((hz K c d).mp h)
  | succ n ih =>
    obtain ⟨e, hstep, hK, hr⟩ := (hs K n c d).mp h
    exact (hs L n c d).mpr ⟨e, hstep, hK.trans hKL, ih hr⟩

/-- A run whose steps each touch at most three cells has a finite footprint.

**Proof sketch.** Induct on the run length using its zero and successor equations. In
the successor case unite the first step's footprint with the recursively obtained set.
`Finset.card_union_le` gives the cardinal bound and `footprint_mono` enlarges the tail. -/
private theorem exists_footprint {C : Type} {S : C → C → Prop} {F : C → Finset ℤ}
    {R : ℕ → C → C → Prop} {RI : Set ℤ → ℕ → C → C → Prop}
    (hz : ∀ c d, R 0 c d ↔ c = d)
    (hs : ∀ n c d, R (n + 1) c d ↔ ∃ e, S c e ∧ R n e d)
    (hIz : ∀ K c d, RI K 0 c d ↔ c = d)
    (hIs : ∀ K n c d, RI K (n + 1) c d ↔
      ∃ e, S c e ∧ ↑(F c) ⊆ K ∧ RI K n e d)
    (hcard : ∀ c, (F c).card ≤ 3) {n : ℕ} {c d : C} (h : R n c d) :
    ∃ K : Finset ℤ, K.card ≤ 3 * n ∧ RI (↑K) n c d := by
  induction n generalizing c with
  | zero =>
    obtain rfl := (hz c d).mp h
    exact ⟨∅, by simp only [Finset.card_empty, Nat.mul_zero, le_refl], (hIz _ _ _).mpr rfl⟩
  | succ n ih =>
    obtain ⟨e, hstep, hr⟩ := (hs n c d).mp h
    obtain ⟨L, hL, hrL⟩ := ih hr
    refine ⟨F c ∪ L, ?_, (hIs _ n c d).mpr ⟨e, hstep, ?_, ?_⟩⟩
    · have hc := Finset.card_union_le (F c) L
      have hF := hcard c
      omega
    · intro a ha
      exact Finset.mem_union.mpr (Or.inl ha)
    · exact footprint_mono hIz hIs hrL
        (fun _ ha => Finset.mem_union.mpr (Or.inr ha))

section Footprints

variable {W : ℕ} {K K' : Set ℤ}

/-- A run within a footprint is a `WordRAM.Steps` run, the footprint forgotten.

**Proof sketch.** Apply `lift_run` with the identity configuration map, dropping the
footprint part of the step relation. Read the target run through `Steps.zero_iff` and
`Steps.succ_iff`. -/
theorem StepsIn.toSteps {P : List Instr} {n : ℕ} {c c' : Cfg W} (h : StepsIn P K n c c') :
    Steps P n c c' := by
  exact lift_run (S := fun c d => step P c = .inl d ∧
      ↑(baseTouches (P.getD c.pc .reject) c.mem) ⊆ K)
    (T := fun c d => step P c = .inl d) (R := StepsIn P K) (R' := Steps P)
    (fun _ _ => Iff.rfl) (fun _ _ _ => by simp only [StepsIn, and_assoc])
    (fun _ _ => Steps.zero_iff) (fun _ _ _ => Steps.succ_iff)
    id (fun _ _ hs => hs.1) h

/-- **Every run has a small footprint**: a run of `n` steps of a vendored program stays within a
finite set of at most `3 n` cells, the cells it touches. So `StepsIn` loses nothing.

**Proof sketch.** Apply `exists_footprint` to the vendored step and the
`baseTouches` footprint. `Steps.zero_iff` and `Steps.succ_iff` expose the run, while
`XInstr.card_touches_le` for a base instruction bounds each step by three cells. -/
theorem Steps.exists_stepsIn {P : List Instr} {n : ℕ} {c c' : Cfg W} (h : Steps P n c c') :
    ∃ K : Finset ℤ, K.card ≤ 3 * n ∧ StepsIn P (↑K) n c c' := by
  exact exists_footprint (S := fun c d => step P c = .inl d)
    (F := fun c => baseTouches (P.getD c.pc .reject) c.mem)
    (RI := StepsIn P) (fun _ _ => Steps.zero_iff) (fun _ _ _ => Steps.succ_iff)
    (fun _ _ _ => Iff.rfl) (fun _ _ _ _ => Iff.rfl)
    (fun c => XInstr.card_touches_le (.base _) c.mem) h

/-- A run within a footprint is a run within every larger set of cells.

**Proof sketch.** Apply `footprint_mono`: induction on the run length composes each
first-step inclusion with the larger footprint inclusion. -/
theorem StepsIn.mono {P : List Instr} {n : ℕ} {c c' : Cfg W} (h : StepsIn P K n c c')
    (hK : K ⊆ K') : StepsIn P K' n c c' := by
  exact footprint_mono (S := fun c d => step P c = .inl d)
    (F := fun c => baseTouches (P.getD c.pc .reject) c.mem)
    (fun _ _ _ => Iff.rfl) (fun _ _ _ _ => Iff.rfl) h hK

/-- One run after the other, within the union of the two footprints.

**Proof sketch.** Induct on the first run length. Enlarge the second run to the union
with `StepsIn.mono` in the zero case; in the successor case include the first footprint
in the union and compose the tail by induction. -/
theorem StepsIn.trans {P : List Instr} {n k : ℕ} {c c₁ c₂ : Cfg W}
    (h₁ : StepsIn P K n c c₁) (h₂ : StepsIn P K' k c₁ c₂) :
    StepsIn P (K ∪ K') (n + k) c c₂ := by
  induction n generalizing c with
  | zero =>
    obtain rfl := h₁
    simpa only [Nat.zero_add] using h₂.mono Set.subset_union_right
  | succ n ih =>
    obtain ⟨d, hs, hK, hr⟩ := h₁
    rw [Nat.add_right_comm]
    exact ⟨d, hs, hK.trans Set.subset_union_left, ih hr⟩

/-! ### Private one-step framing -/

/-- A base step changes no cell outside its footprint.

**Proof sketch.** Read the transition through `step_def`. Each writing arm is an update
at a cell listed by `baseTouches`, so `AgreeOutside.update` applies to
`AgreeOutside.refl`. The branch leaves the memory unchanged, and verdicts cannot step. -/
private theorem step_agreeOutside {P : List Instr} {c d : Cfg W}
    (hs : step P c = .inl d)
    (hK : ↑(baseTouches (P.getD c.pc .reject) c.mem) ⊆ K) :
    AgreeOutside K c.mem d.mem := by
  have write (a : ℤ) (v : BitVec W) (ha : a ∈ K) :
      AgreeOutside K c.mem (fun z => if z = a then v else c.mem z) := by
    have he : (fun z => if z = a then v else c.mem z) = Function.update c.mem a v := by
      funext z
      exact (Function.update_apply c.mem a v z).symm
    rw [he]
    exact (AgreeOutside.refl K c.mem).update ha v
  cases hi : P.getD c.pc .reject <;>
    simp only [step_def, hi, Sum.inl.injEq, Sum.inr_ne_inl] at hs
  all_goals
    subst d
    simp only [hi, baseTouches] at hK
    first | exact AgreeOutside.refl K c.mem
          | exact write _ _ (hK (by simp))

/-- **Every intermediate memory stays inside the footprint** (design §15c, obstacle 1). Along a
run within `K`, the configuration reached after any `k ≤ n` steps splits the run into two runs
within `K`, and its memory agrees with the starting memory outside `K`. The vendored `Finished`
and `compileProgram_correct` speak of the last memory only.

**Proof sketch.** Induct on the prefix length. Split off the first step and
apply induction to the remaining run. `step_agreeOutside` frames that step by
`AgreeOutside.update`, including the indirect target of a store; `AgreeOutside.trans`
composes this frame with that of the remaining prefix. -/
theorem StepsIn.agreeOutside {P : List Instr} {n : ℕ} {c c' : Cfg W}
    (h : StepsIn P K n c c') {k : ℕ} (hk : k ≤ n) :
    ∃ d : Cfg W, StepsIn P K k c d ∧ StepsIn P K (n - k) d c' ∧ AgreeOutside K c.mem d.mem := by
  induction k generalizing n c with
  | zero => exact ⟨c, rfl, h, AgreeOutside.refl K c.mem⟩
  | succ k ih =>
    cases n with
    | zero => omega
    | succ n =>
      obtain ⟨d, hs, hK, hr⟩ := h
      obtain ⟨e, hp, ht, hm⟩ := ih hr (Nat.le_of_succ_le_succ hk)
      refine ⟨e, ⟨d, hs, hK, hp⟩, ?_, (step_agreeOutside hs hK).trans hm⟩
      simpa only [Nat.add_sub_add_right] using ht

/-- **Straight-line code with its footprint.** A list of straight-line instructions runs through
in its length, as in `WordRAM.steps_straight`, within any `K` that contains what each instruction
touches in every memory. For `load` and `store` the hypothesis asks `K` to contain every address
a word can hold, so in practice this row serves code without them; loads and stores take the
known-number rows `steps_load`/`steps_store`, with their address added to the footprint.

**Proof sketch.** Induct on the instruction list. Obtain the first transition by
specializing `steps_straight` to the singleton head, using `CodeAt.left` and the published
`Steps` equations. Add the footprint inclusion from `hK` and recurse on `CodeAt.tail`;
`effects_cons` identifies the final memory. -/
theorem stepsIn_straight {P : List Instr} (l : List Instr) (pc : ℕ) (m : ℤ → BitVec W)
    (hcode : CodeAt P pc l) (hl : ∀ i ∈ l, Straight i)
    (hK : ∀ i ∈ l, ∀ m' : ℤ → BitVec W, ↑(baseTouches i m') ⊆ K) :
    StepsIn P K l.length ⟨pc, m⟩ ⟨pc + l.length, effects l m⟩ := by
  induction l generalizing pc m with
  | nil => simp only [List.length_nil, Nat.add_zero, effects_nil, StepsIn]
  | cons i l ih =>
    obtain ⟨hi, hl⟩ := List.forall_mem_cons.mp hl
    have hfirst : Steps P 1 ⟨pc, m⟩ ⟨pc + 1, effect i m⟩ := by
      simpa only [List.length_singleton, effects_cons, effects_nil] using
        (steps_straight [i] pc m (CodeAt.left (l₂ := l) hcode)
          (by simpa only [List.forall_mem_singleton] using hi))
    obtain ⟨d, hs, he⟩ := Steps.succ_iff.mp hfirst
    obtain rfl := Steps.zero_iff.mp he
    simp only [List.length_cons, effects_cons]
    refine ⟨⟨pc + 1, effect i m⟩, hs, ?_, ?_⟩
    · rw [hcode.head]
      exact hK i (List.mem_cons_self) m
    · have ht := ih (pc + 1) (effect i m) hcode.tail hl
        (fun j hj m' => hK j (List.mem_cons_of_mem i hj) m')
      convert ht using 1; congr 1; omega

/-- An extended run within a footprint is an `XSteps` run, the footprint forgotten.

**Proof sketch.** Apply `lift_run` with the identity configuration map, dropping
only the footprint inclusion from each extended step. -/
theorem XStepsIn.toXSteps {P : XProgram} {x ρ : List Bool} {n : ℕ} {c c' : XCfg W}
    (h : XStepsIn P x ρ K n c c') : XSteps P x ρ n c c' := by
  exact lift_run (S := fun c d => xstep P x ρ c = .inl d ∧
      ↑((P.getD c.pc (.base .reject)).touches c.mem) ⊆ K)
    (T := fun c d => xstep P x ρ c = .inl d) (R := XStepsIn P x ρ K)
    (R' := XSteps P x ρ) (fun _ _ => Iff.rfl)
    (fun _ _ _ => by simp only [XStepsIn, and_assoc])
    (fun _ _ => Iff.rfl) (fun _ _ _ => Iff.rfl) id (fun _ _ hs => hs.1) h

/-- **Every extended run has a small footprint**: a run of `n` steps stays within a finite set of
at most `3 n` cells, the cells it touches.

**Proof sketch.** Apply the shared `exists_footprint` construction to
`xstep` and `XInstr.touches`. `XInstr.card_touches_le` bounds each step, and
`Finset.card_union_le` bounds their accumulated union. -/
theorem XSteps.exists_xStepsIn {P : XProgram} {x ρ : List Bool} {n : ℕ} {c c' : XCfg W}
    (h : XSteps P x ρ n c c') : ∃ K : Finset ℤ, K.card ≤ 3 * n ∧ XStepsIn P x ρ (↑K) n c c' := by
  exact exists_footprint (S := fun c d => xstep P x ρ c = .inl d)
    (F := fun c => (P.getD c.pc (.base .reject)).touches c.mem)
    (RI := XStepsIn P x ρ) (fun _ _ => Iff.rfl) (fun _ _ _ => Iff.rfl)
    (fun _ _ _ => Iff.rfl) (fun _ _ _ _ => Iff.rfl)
    (fun c => XInstr.card_touches_le _ c.mem) h

/-- The footprint form of `XSteps.of_steps_proj`: a verdict-free run of the projection within `K`
is a run of the extended program within `K`.

**Proof sketch.** Use `lift_run` to preserve both the transition and its
footprint. `projected_fetch` identifies the fetched base instruction, and
`xstep_base_fetch` identifies its transition; its `XInstr.touches` is the projected
instruction's `baseTouches`. -/
theorem XStepsIn.of_stepsIn_proj {P : XProgram} {x ρ : List Bool} {n : ℕ}
    {c c' : Cfg W} (h : StepsIn P.proj K n c c') (o : List Bool) (r : ℕ) :
    XStepsIn P x ρ K n ⟨c, o, r⟩ ⟨c', o, r⟩ := by
  refine lift_run (S := fun c d => step P.proj c = .inl d ∧
      ↑(baseTouches (P.proj.getD c.pc .reject) c.mem) ⊆ K)
    (T := fun c d => xstep P x ρ c = .inl d ∧
      ↑((P.getD c.pc (.base .reject)).touches c.mem) ⊆ K)
    (R := StepsIn P.proj K) (R' := XStepsIn P x ρ K)
    (fun _ _ => Iff.rfl) (fun _ _ _ => by simp only [StepsIn, and_assoc])
    (fun _ _ => Iff.rfl) (fun _ _ _ => by simp only [XStepsIn, and_assoc])
    (fun d => (⟨d, o, r⟩ : XCfg W)) (fun c d hs => ?_) h
  obtain ⟨hs, hK⟩ := hs
  have hf := projected_fetch hs
  constructor
  · change xstep P x ρ ⟨c, o, r⟩ = .inl ⟨d, o, r⟩
    rw [xstep_base_fetch x ρ c o r hf, hs, Sum.map_inl]
  · change ↑((P.getD c.pc (.base .reject)).touches c.mem) ⊆ K
    rw [hf]
    exact hK

end Footprints

/-! ## Starting and halting -/

/-- **The memory in which a run starts**, on an input of length `n`: the cell `-1` holds `n`
(as a word, so modulo `2^W`) and every other cell holds `0`. The cell `-1` is the compiler's
first argument cell (`Light.Compiler.cARG1`, `Light/Compiler/StatementCode.lean:82`), so a
compiled program receives `|x|` as its first argument (design §15c: `inLen` from an argument
cell, no `len` instruction). -/
def initMem (W n : ℕ) : ℤ → BitVec W :=
  Function.update (fun _ => 0) (-1) (wd W n)

/-- The start configuration on an input of length `n`: position `0`, `initMem W n`, nothing
emitted, no coin consumed. -/
def XCfg.init (W n : ℕ) : XCfg W := { pc := 0, mem := initMem W n, out := [], rd := 0 }

/-- **Halting within steps and cells** (stage 2's hypothesis). `HaltsWithin P x ρ W t v o f`: run
on the input `x` with the coin stream `ρ` and word size `W` from `XCfg.init W |x|`, the program
`P` gives the verdict `v` within `t` steps, the verdict step counted as in `WordRAM.exec`; it has
then emitted `o`; and every step before the verdict stays within a set of at most `f` cells. -/
def HaltsWithin (P : XProgram) (x ρ : List Bool) (W t : ℕ) (v : Bool) (o : List Bool) (f : ℕ) :
    Prop :=
  ∃ (n : ℕ) (c : XCfg W) (K : Finset ℤ), n < t ∧ K.card ≤ f ∧
    XStepsIn P x ρ (↑K) n (XCfg.init W x.length) c ∧ xstep P x ρ c = .inr v ∧ c.out = o

section Halting

variable {P : XProgram} {x ρ : List Bool} {W t t' f f' : ℕ} {v v' : Bool} {o o' : List Bool}

/-- A run that halts within `t` steps and `f` cells halts within any larger bounds.

**Proof sketch.** Keep the same witnesses, composing `n < t` with `t ≤ t'` and
the footprint cardinal bound with `f ≤ f'`. -/
theorem HaltsWithin.mono (h : HaltsWithin P x ρ W t v o f) (ht : t ≤ t') (hf : f ≤ f') :
    HaltsWithin P x ρ W t' v o f' := by
  obtain ⟨n, c, K, hn, hK, hr, hv, ho⟩ := h
  exact ⟨n, c, K, hn.trans_le ht, hK.trans hf, hr, hv, ho⟩

/-- **A bound in steps gives one in cells**: a run that halts within `t` steps halts within `t`
steps touching at most `3 t` cells. So for time bounds the cell count needs no separate proof.

**Proof sketch.** Forget the footprint with `XStepsIn.toXSteps`, then use
`XSteps.exists_xStepsIn` to obtain at most `3 * n` cells. Multiply `n < t` by three
to place that footprint within the required bound. -/
theorem HaltsWithin.touches_le (h : HaltsWithin P x ρ W t v o f) :
    HaltsWithin P x ρ W t v o (3 * t) := by
  obtain ⟨n, c, _, hn, _, hr, hv, ho⟩ := h
  obtain ⟨K, hK, hrun⟩ := hr.toXSteps.exists_xStepsIn
  exact ⟨n, c, K, hn, hK.trans (Nat.mul_le_mul_left 3 hn.le), hrun, hv, ho⟩

/-! ### Private halting-run determinism -/

/-- Two runs from one configuration that end in verdicts have the same last configuration
and verdict.

**Proof sketch.** Induct on the first length and case on the second. A zero-length run
already gives a verdict, so a positive-length competitor contradicts the disjointness of
`Sum.inl` and `Sum.inr`. If both lengths are positive, injectivity of `Sum.inl` identifies
the first successors and the induction hypothesis identifies the tails. -/
private theorem xSteps_verdict_unique {n k : ℕ} {c d e : XCfg W}
    (h₁ : XSteps P x ρ n c d) (hv : xstep P x ρ d = .inr v)
    (h₂ : XSteps P x ρ k c e) (hv' : xstep P x ρ e = .inr v') :
    d = e ∧ v = v' := by
  induction n generalizing c k with
  | zero =>
    obtain rfl := h₁
    cases k with
    | zero =>
      obtain rfl := h₂
      exact ⟨rfl, Sum.inr.inj (hv.symm.trans hv')⟩
    | succ k =>
      obtain ⟨a, hs, _⟩ := h₂
      have bad := hv.symm.trans hs
      contradiction
  | succ n ih =>
    obtain ⟨a, hs, hr⟩ := h₁
    cases k with
    | zero =>
      obtain rfl := h₂
      have bad := hv'.symm.trans hs
      contradiction
    | succ k =>
      obtain ⟨b, hs', hr'⟩ := h₂
      have he : a = b := Sum.inl.inj (hs.symm.trans hs')
      subst b
      exact ih hr hr'

/-- **The run is determined** by the input, the coin stream and the word size: two halting runs
give the same verdict and the same output.

**Proof sketch.** Forget the footprints and apply `xSteps_verdict_unique`.
That helper synchronizes the two runs using injectivity of `Sum.inl` and excludes
stepping past a verdict. Equality of the last configurations identifies their outputs. -/
theorem HaltsWithin.unique (h : HaltsWithin P x ρ W t v o f)
    (h' : HaltsWithin P x ρ W t' v' o' f') : v = v' ∧ o = o' := by
  obtain ⟨_, c, _, _, _, hr, hv, ho⟩ := h
  obtain ⟨_, d, _, _, _, hr', hv', ho'⟩ := h'
  obtain ⟨he, hvv⟩ := xSteps_verdict_unique hr.toXSteps hv hr'.toXSteps hv'
  exact ⟨hvv, ho.symm.trans ((congrArg XCfg.out he).trans ho')⟩

/-! ### Private coin-free transition -/

/-- A coin-free program takes the same step for every coin stream. -/
private theorem xstep_coin_irrel (hP : CoinFree P) (ρ' : List Bool) (c : XCfg W) :
    xstep P x ρ c = xstep P x ρ' c := by
  have hnot : ∀ a, P.getD c.pc (.base .reject) ≠ .coin a := by
    intro a ha
    rw [List.getD_eq_getElem?_getD, Option.getD_eq_iff] at ha
    rcases ha with hm | ⟨_, he⟩
    · exact hP (.coin a) (List.mem_of_getElem? hm) a rfl
    · cases he
  cases hi : P.getD c.pc (.base .reject) <;> simp only [xstep, hi]
  case base i => cases i <;> rfl
  case coin i => exact (hnot i hi).elim

/-- **A coin-free program does not depend on the coin stream.**

**Proof sketch.** `xstep_coin_irrel` excludes a fetched coin instruction
using `CoinFree` and `List.mem_of_getElem?`. Apply `lift_run` with the identity map to
transport each footprint run between coin streams, preserving the final verdict and output.
The same transport proves both directions. -/
theorem HaltsWithin.coin_irrel (hP : CoinFree P) (ρ' : List Bool) :
    HaltsWithin P x ρ W t v o f ↔ HaltsWithin P x ρ' W t v o f := by
  have transport (σ τ : List Bool) (h : HaltsWithin P x σ W t v o f) :
      HaltsWithin P x τ W t v o f := by
    obtain ⟨n, c, K, hn, hK, hr, hv, ho⟩ := h
    refine ⟨n, c, K, hn, hK, ?_, (xstep_coin_irrel hP τ c).symm.trans hv, ho⟩
    exact lift_run (S := fun a b => xstep P x σ a = .inl b ∧
        ↑((P.getD a.pc (.base .reject)).touches a.mem) ⊆ (↑K : Set ℤ))
      (T := fun a b => xstep P x τ a = .inl b ∧
        ↑((P.getD a.pc (.base .reject)).touches a.mem) ⊆ (↑K : Set ℤ))
      (R := XStepsIn P x σ (↑K)) (R' := XStepsIn P x τ (↑K))
      (fun _ _ => Iff.rfl) (fun _ _ _ => by simp only [XStepsIn, and_assoc])
      (fun _ _ => Iff.rfl) (fun _ _ _ => by simp only [XStepsIn, and_assoc])
      id (fun a _ hs => ⟨(xstep_coin_irrel hP τ a).symm.trans hs.1, hs.2⟩) hr
  exact ⟨transport ρ ρ', transport ρ' ρ⟩

end Halting

/-! ## Readers

Equations for the definitions above, so that other files read them without unfolding them. Each
restates its definition and is proved by `rfl` or `Iff.rfl`. -/

section Readers

variable {W : ℕ}

/-- The word of an input bit, as an equation: `1` if `0 ≤ p` and the bit of `x` at `p` is
`true`, and `0` otherwise. -/
theorem inBitWord_def (x : List Bool) (p : ℤ) :
    inBitWord W x p = if 0 ≤ p ∧ x.getD p.toNat false = true then 1 else 0 := rfl

/-- The word of a coin, as an equation: `1` for `true`, `0` for `false`. -/
theorem coinWord_def (b : Bool) : coinWord W b = if b then 1 else 0 := rfl

/-- **One step of the extended RAM, as an equation**: the match on the instruction at `c.pc`
(`WordRAM.reject` past the end). -/
theorem xstep_def (P : XProgram) (x ρ : List Bool) (c : XCfg W) :
    xstep P x ρ c =
      match P.getD c.pc (.base .reject) with
      | .base .accept => .inr true
      | .base .reject => .inr false
      | .base (.bltz i l) =>
        .inl { c with pc := if (c.mem i).toInt < 0 then l else c.pc + 1 }
      | .base ins => .inl { c with pc := c.pc + 1, mem := effect ins c.mem }
      | .rdIn i j =>
        .inl { c with pc := c.pc + 1,
                      mem := Function.update c.mem i (inBitWord W x (c.mem j).toInt) }
      | .emit i => .inl { c with pc := c.pc + 1, out := c.out ++ [decide (c.mem i ≠ 0)] }
      | .coin i =>
        .inl { c with pc := c.pc + 1,
                      mem := Function.update c.mem i (coinWord W (ρ.getD c.rd false)),
                      rd := c.rd + 1 }
      | .half i j =>
        .inl { c with pc := c.pc + 1, mem := Function.update c.mem i ((c.mem j).sshiftRight 1) }
    := rfl

/-- A run of no steps stays where it is. -/
theorem XSteps.zero_iff {P : XProgram} {x ρ : List Bool} {c c' : XCfg W} :
    XSteps P x ρ 0 c c' ↔ c = c' := Iff.rfl

/-- A run of `n + 1` steps is a first step to some `c₁`, giving no verdict, followed by a run of
`n` steps from `c₁`. -/
theorem XSteps.succ_iff {P : XProgram} {x ρ : List Bool} {n : ℕ} {c c' : XCfg W} :
    XSteps P x ρ (n + 1) c c' ↔ ∃ c₁, xstep P x ρ c = .inl c₁ ∧ XSteps P x ρ n c₁ c' := Iff.rfl

/-- The cells a vendored instruction touches, as an equation. -/
theorem baseTouches_def (ins : Instr) (m : ℤ → BitVec W) :
    baseTouches ins m =
      match ins with
      | .one i => {i}
      | .add i j k => {i, j, k}
      | .sub i j k => {i, j, k}
      | .mul i j k => {i, j, k}
      | .load i j => {i, j, (m j).toInt}
      | .store i j => {i, j, (m i).toInt}
      | .bltz i _ => {i}
      | .accept => ∅
      | .reject => ∅ := rfl

/-- The cells an instruction of the extended RAM touches, as an equation. -/
theorem XInstr.touches_def (ins : XInstr) (m : ℤ → BitVec W) :
    ins.touches m =
      match ins with
      | .base ins => baseTouches ins m
      | .rdIn i j => {i, j}
      | .emit i => {i}
      | .coin i => {i}
      | .half i j => {i, j} := by
  cases ins <;> rfl

/-- A run within `K` of no steps stays where it is. -/
theorem XStepsIn.zero_iff {P : XProgram} {x ρ : List Bool} {K : Set ℤ} {c c' : XCfg W} :
    XStepsIn P x ρ K 0 c c' ↔ c = c' := Iff.rfl

/-- A run within `K` of `n + 1` steps is a first step to some `c₁`, giving no verdict and
touching only cells of `K`, followed by a run within `K` of `n` steps from `c₁`. -/
theorem XStepsIn.succ_iff {P : XProgram} {x ρ : List Bool} {K : Set ℤ} {n : ℕ}
    {c c' : XCfg W} :
    XStepsIn P x ρ K (n + 1) c c' ↔ ∃ c₁, xstep P x ρ c = .inl c₁ ∧
      ↑((P.getD c.pc (.base .reject)).touches c.mem) ⊆ K ∧ XStepsIn P x ρ K n c₁ c' := Iff.rfl

/-- The memory in which a run starts, as an equation: `n` (as a word) at the cell `-1` and `0`
everywhere else. -/
theorem initMem_def (n : ℕ) : initMem W n = Function.update (fun _ => 0) (-1) (wd W n) := rfl

/-- The start configuration, as an equation. -/
theorem XCfg.init_def (n : ℕ) :
    XCfg.init W n = { pc := 0, mem := initMem W n, out := [], rd := 0 } := rfl

/-- Halting within steps and cells, as an equivalence with its definition. -/
theorem haltsWithin_iff (P : XProgram) (x ρ : List Bool) (t : ℕ) (v : Bool) (o : List Bool)
    (f : ℕ) :
    HaltsWithin P x ρ W t v o f ↔
      ∃ (n : ℕ) (c : XCfg W) (K : Finset ℤ), n < t ∧ K.card ≤ f ∧
        XStepsIn P x ρ (↑K) n (XCfg.init W x.length) c ∧ xstep P x ρ c = .inr v ∧ c.out = o :=
  Iff.rfl

end Readers

end WordRAM
```

## ===== TCSlib/Complexity/Randomized/Classes.lean =====

```
/-
Copyright (c) 2026 The TCSlib Authors. All rights reserved.
Released under Apache 2.0 license as described in the file LICENSE.
Authors: TCSlib Contributors
-/
import Mathlib.Computability.Language
import Mathlib.Data.Fintype.Pi
import Mathlib.Data.Fintype.BigOperators
import Mathlib.Data.Rat.Defs
import Mathlib.Data.Nat.Choose.Sum
import Mathlib.Algebra.Order.Field.Basic
import Mathlib.Algebra.BigOperators.Ring.Finset
import Mathlib.Logic.Equiv.Fin.Basic
import Mathlib.Data.List.OfFn
import Mathlib.Tactic.Positivity
import Mathlib.Tactic.FieldSimp
import Mathlib.Tactic.Linarith
import Mathlib.Tactic.Ring

set_option maxHeartbeats 0
set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
set_option autoImplicit false

/-!
# The randomized complexity classes BPP, RP, coRP, ZPP

Verifier-style definitions of Arora–Barak's randomized complexity classes,
following the certificate view of [AB09, Def 7.3]: a language is in a
randomized class when some efficient two-input predicate `M(x, r)`, run on the
input `x` and a uniformly random string `r` of polynomially-bounded length,
decides membership with the class's acceptance-probability profile.

## Main definitions

* `Randomized.randProb` — the probability of an event over a uniform random
  string of a given length, as a rational counting ratio.
* `Randomized.polyLen` — the canonical polynomial length schedule
  `n ↦ a·(n+1)^k` for random strings.
* `Randomized.VerifierModel` — an abstract *efficiency notion* standing in for
  "polynomial-time Turing machine" (see **Deviations** below).
* `Randomized.InBPP` — [AB09, Def 7.3] (equivalently Def 7.2 via the
  certificate view).
* `Randomized.InRP`, `Randomized.InCoRP` — [AB09, Def 7.6] and the remark
  following it.
* `Randomized.InZPP` — [AB09, Def 7.7], in the zero-error "abort"
  formulation (see **Deviations**).
* `Randomized.raceVerifier`, `Randomized.majorityVerifier` — the two verifier
  constructions used by Theorems 7.8 and 7.10.

## Main results

* `Randomized.inZPP_iff_inRP_and_inCoRP` — `ZPP = RP ∩ coRP` [AB09, Thm 7.8].
* `Randomized.InBPP.compl` — `BPP = coBPP` (used by [AB09, Thm 7.15]).
* `Randomized.bpp_error_reduction` — error reduction [AB09, Thm 7.10].
* `Randomized.inBPPWeak_iff_inBPP` — `BPP_{n^{-c}} = BPP` [AB09, Lem 7.9].

## Deviations from the source

* **Machine model.** [AB09] defines these classes with polynomial-time Turing
  machines.  Per the repository's agreed scope, we use the certificate view of
  [AB09, Def 7.3] — predicates over inputs and random strings — and replace
  "`M` is a polynomial-time TM" by membership in an abstract
  `VerifierModel` `E`, a predicate on verifiers.  Every class and theorem is
  parametrized by `E`.  The closure properties that [AB09]'s proofs use
  (building the race, majority, complement, and projection verifiers out of
  given ones) are stated as explicit named hypotheses (`ClosedUnder…`), all of
  which hold for the intended instantiation "computable in polynomial time".
  That instantiation is `Randomized.polyTimeModel` (`Randomized/PolyTimeModel.lean`),
  which discharges the closure hypotheses; `Randomized/BPPAmplify.lean` instantiates
  Lemma 7.9 and Theorem 7.10 there and transfers them to polynomial-time PTMs.
* **Length schedules.** [AB09, Def 7.3] draws `r ∈ {0,1}^{p(|x|)}` for a
  polynomial `p`.  We fix the canonical schedule `polyLen a k : n ↦ a·(n+1)^k`
  (existentially quantified over `a, k : ℕ`) rather than an arbitrary
  `p : ℕ → ℕ` with a polynomial bound: an arbitrary bounded `p` need not be
  computable and could smuggle undecidable information through the schedule
  itself.  Every polynomial is dominated by some `polyLen a k`, and a verifier
  can ignore padding bits, so the class is unchanged.
* **Probability.** `Pr_{r ∈ {0,1}^m}` is the counting ratio
  `#{r : accepted}/2^m` valued in `ℚ`; random strings of length `m` are
  `Fin m → Bool`, passed to verifiers as lists via `List.ofFn`.
* **ZPP.** [AB09, Def 7.7] defines `ZPP` by expected running time of a
  zero-error machine.  Expected time is not expressible for abstract
  predicates, so we use the standard equivalent "Las Vegas" formulation: a
  verifier with values in `Option Bool` that is never wrong and outputs `none`
  ("don't know") with probability at most `1/2`.  [AB09, §7.4.2] sketches the
  equivalence of expected-time and worst-case formulations (truncation via
  Markov's inequality).
* **The weak threshold of Lemma 7.9.** The book's success threshold
  `1/2 + |x|^{-c}` exceeds `1` for `|x| ≤ 1`, making the literal class
  `BPP_{n^{-c}}` empty; we require advantage `min (1/6) ((|x|+1)^{-c})`
  instead, which for `c ≥ 1` agrees with the book's (up to the `n+1` shift) for
  `|x| ≥ 5` and makes `BPP ⊆ BPP_{n^{-c}}` hold as the book intends.
* The constant `2/3` follows [AB09, Defs 7.2/7.6]; `1/2` in `InZPP` is the
  conventional choice (any constant in `(0,1)` gives the same class, by the
  same repetition argument as [AB09, §7.4.1]).

## References

* [AB09] S. Arora, B. Barak, *Computational Complexity: A Modern Approach*,
  Cambridge University Press, 2009.
-/

namespace Randomized

open Finset

/-- The probability, over a uniformly random string `r ∈ {0,1}^m`, that the
event `A` holds of `r` (as a list): the counting ratio `#{r : A r}/2^m`.
[AB09, Def 7.3: "`Pr_{r ∈_R {0,1}^{p(|x|)}}`"] -/
def randProb (m : ℕ) (A : List Bool → Prop) [DecidablePred A] : ℚ :=
  ((univ.filter fun r : Fin m → Bool => A (List.ofFn r)).card : ℚ) / 2 ^ m

/-- Two events that agree on every random string have the same
probability. -/
theorem randProb_congr {m : ℕ} {A B : List Bool → Prop} [DecidablePred A]
    [DecidablePred B]
    (h : ∀ r : Fin m → Bool, A (List.ofFn r) ↔ B (List.ofFn r)) :
    randProb m A = randProb m B := by
  unfold randProb
  rw [Finset.filter_congr fun r _ => h r]

/-- Probabilities are nonnegative. -/
theorem randProb_nonneg {m : ℕ} {A : List Bool → Prop} [DecidablePred A] :
    0 ≤ randProb m A := by
  unfold randProb
  positivity

/-- There are `2^m` random strings of length `m`. -/
theorem card_univ_bitstrings (m : ℕ) :
    (univ : Finset (Fin m → Bool)).card = 2 ^ m := by
  rw [Finset.card_univ, Fintype.card_fun, Fintype.card_bool, Fintype.card_fin]

/-- Probabilities are at most one. -/
theorem randProb_le_one {m : ℕ} {A : List Bool → Prop} [DecidablePred A] :
    randProb m A ≤ 1 := by
  unfold randProb
  rw [div_le_one (by positivity)]
  calc ((univ.filter fun r : Fin m → Bool => A (List.ofFn r)).card : ℚ)
      ≤ ((univ : Finset (Fin m → Bool)).card : ℚ) := by
        exact_mod_cast Finset.card_filter_le _ _
    _ = 2 ^ m := by rw [card_univ_bitstrings]; push_cast; rfl

/-- Probability is monotone in the event. -/
theorem randProb_mono {m : ℕ} {A B : List Bool → Prop} [DecidablePred A]
    [DecidablePred B]
    (h : ∀ r : Fin m → Bool, A (List.ofFn r) → B (List.ofFn r)) :
    randProb m A ≤ randProb m B := by
  unfold randProb
  gcongr
  exact h _

/-- Complement rule: `Pr[¬A] = 1 − Pr[A]`. -/
theorem randProb_not {m : ℕ} (A : List Bool → Prop) [DecidablePred A] :
    randProb m (fun l => ¬ A l) = 1 - randProb m A := by
  have hsplit := Finset.filter_card_add_filter_neg_card_eq_card
    (s := (univ : Finset (Fin m → Bool))) (p := fun r => A (List.ofFn r))
  rw [card_univ_bitstrings] at hsplit
